## Project 0035: Interactive Motors (Direction)

### 2. Learning Objective
Control direction. You will use an **H-Bridge** driver to spin basic motors Forward and Backward.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **H-Bridge**: A circuit that can reverse polarity.
*   **Logic States**: A=High, B=Low (Fwd); A=Low, B=High (Rev).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button A, Button B**
*   **DC Motor + H-Bridge Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Btn Fwd** | GP10 | Input to trigger Forward spin |
| **Btn Rev** | GP11 | Input to trigger Backward spin |
| **Driver IA** | GP14 | Direction Control A |
| **Driver IB** | GP15 | Direction Control B |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power for Motor |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else if / else)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btnF**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for forward control.
*   **btnR**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(11, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for reverse control.
*   **ia**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` for H-Bridge Input A.
*   **ib**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)` for H-Bridge Input B.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btnF`**, **`btnR`**, **`ia`**, and **`ib`**.
    *   Set **`btnF`** and **`btnR`** as **Inputs** with **Pull-Down**.
    *   Set **`ia`** and **`ib`** as **Outputs**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Direction Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a multi-branch **`controls_if`** block. Click the gear to add `else if` and `else` branches.
    *   **Branch 1 (Forward)**: If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btnF`** equals **1**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (ia) to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (ib) to **LOW (0)**.
    *   **Branch 2 (Reverse)**: Else If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btnR`** equals **1**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (ia) to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (ib) to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Branch 3 (Stop)**: Else:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (ia) to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (ib) to **LOW (0)**.
4.  **Loop Stability**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.01** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
To go forward, we push current from A to B. To go backward, we push from B to A. If we pull both to Ground, the motor stops.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
ia = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
ib = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
btnF = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btnR = Pin(11, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    if btnF.value():
        # Spin Forward
        ia.value(1)
        ib.value(0)
    elif btnR.value():
        # Spin Reverse
        ia.value(0)
        ib.value(1)
    else:
        # Stop
        ia.value(0)
        ib.value(0)
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Shoot-Through (Both High)**: Never set both IA and IB to 1 at the same time on simple drivers. This may cause the H-Bridge to short the power supply and GND internally, generating massive heat.
*   **Weak Ground**: If the motor jitters or the buttons don't seem to work, check that the Driver's GND is firmly connected to the Pico's GND.
*   **Power Limits**: DC motors draw high peak currents. If the Pico disconnects when the motor starts, your USB power source is tripping due to the current spike. Use an external battery for the H-Bridge VCC.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Electronic Braking**: If your driver supports it, set both IA and IB to HIGH to "short" the motor coils, making the shaft stop much faster than coasting.
*   **Hybrid Speed**: Combine this with PWM logic to have forward and reverse modes with adjustable speeds.
*   **Remote Control**: Trigger the directions using a joystick or an IR remote instead of physical buttons.

## Project 0036: Smart Motor Switch (Garage Door)

### 2. Learning Objective
Automate duration control. The motor runs for a fixed time then stops, simulating a garage door interacting with a limit (time-based limit).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Open Loop Control**: Assuming the door opens in X seconds without verifying position.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **DC Motor + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Door Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Driver IN-A** | GP14 | Motor Control Signal |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power Supply |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] and [Y]`** (logic_operation)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`not`** (logic_negate)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`false`** (logic_boolean)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **isOpening**: Boolean variable used as a "latch" to prevent multiple triggers during the sequence.
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for the trigger button.
*   **motor**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` to drive the garage door motor.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`motor`**, and **`isOpening`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`motor`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`isOpening`** to **false**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Check Trigger Condition**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btn`** equals **1** AND **`isOpening`** equals **false** (drag **`[X] and [Y]`** and **`not`** from **Logic & Math**).
4.  **Execute Open Sequence**:
    *   Inside the "if" block:
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [isOpening] to`**. From **Logic & Math**, drag **`true`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds (Wait for door to physically open).
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [isOpening] to`**. From **Logic & Math**, drag **`false`**.
5.  **Loop Stability**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.01** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
One click triggers the entire 5-second sequence. You don't need to hold the button. The system takes over until the task is done.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
motor = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
isOpening = False

while True:
    # Trigger only if button is pressed AND system is not already running
    if btn.value() and not isOpening:
        isOpening = True
        motor.value(1)  # Power ON
        time.sleep(5)   # Wait for physical motion
        motor.value(0)  # Power OFF
        isOpening = False
        
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Open Loop Uncertainty**: This system is "Open Loop," meaning it assumes the door is open after 5 seconds but doesn't actually know. If the door gets stuck, the motor will keep straining until the timer finishes.
*   **Trigger Spam**: Without the `isOpening` flag, pressing the button twice would restart the 5-second timer while the motor is already running, which can lead to unpredictable timing.
*   **Power Failure**: If power fails mid-open, the code won't know the door is half-way. Professional systems use limit switches (Section 5) to solve this.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Safety Stop**: Modify the code so that if the button is pressed *while* `isOpening` is true, the motor stops immediately.
*   **Status Light**: Add an LED that flashes while the door is moving.
*   **Reverse Action**: Add a second button that runs the motor in the opposite direction for 5 seconds to close the door.

## Project 0037: Motor Alarm (Intruder Spray)

### 2. Learning Objective
Trigger mechanical action from sensors. Connect a PIR (Motion) sensor to a pump/fan.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Interfacing**: Connecting Input logic to Output power.
*   **Dwell Time**: Keeping the output on for a minimum time after trigger.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **PIR Motion Sensor**
*   **DC Motor/Pump + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **PIR Sensor** | GP16 | Intruder Detection Input |
| **Driver IN-A** | GP14 | Motor/Pump Control Signal |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power Supply |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **pir**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(16, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` to read the motion sensor.
*   **motor**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` to trigger the alarm/pump.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`pir`** and **`motor`**.
    *   Set **`pir** to **`Pin(16, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`motor`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Check PIR Status**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`pir`** equals **1**.
4.  **Activate Trap Sequence**:
    *   Inside the "if" block:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds. (Action duration).
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds. (Cooldown period).

### 9. Execution Flow
The PIR detects body heat. It sends a signal. The Pico receives it and activates the "Trap" (Motor) for 2 seconds, then resets to waiting mode.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
pir = Pin(16, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
motor = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    if pir.value():
        motor.value(1) # Activate spray/motor
        time.sleep(2)
        motor.value(0)
        time.sleep(5)  # Cooldown before next trigger
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensor Stabilization**: PIR sensors require a "warm-up" period (up to 60 seconds) after power-up. They will often output HIGH randomly during this time.
*   **Range Sensitivity**: PIRs detect body heat. If the motor is placed too close to the sensor, the heat from the motor driver might cause a "False Feedback" loop.
*   **Continuous Triggering**: If you forget a cooldown `wait`, the system might spray dozens of times rapidly because PIR signals often stay HIGH for several seconds after motion stops.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Stealth Mode**: Add an LED that stays OFF while waitng but turns RED during the spray to alert the intruder.
*   **Panic Button**: Add a physical button that triggers the spray regardless of the PIR status for manual defense.
*   **Day/Night Guard**: Use an LDR (Project 0029) so that the intruder alarm only activates when the room is dark.

## Project 0038: The Motor Game (Spin the Wheel)

### 2. Learning Objective
Random actuation. Spin a wheel for a random duration to select a winner.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Random Duration**: `sleep(random)`.
*   **Deceleration**: (Advanced) simulated friction.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **DC Motor (with pointer/wheel) + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Spin Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Driver IN-A** | GP14 | Motor Control Pulse |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power for Spin Motor |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`random integer from [1] to [100]`** (math_random_int)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`repeat while`** (controls_whileUntil)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` to start the game.
*   **motor**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` to drive the spinning wheel.
*   **spinTime**: Number variable storing the randomly generated duration of the spin.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`motor`**, and **`spinTime`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`motor`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Check Button Press**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btn`** equals **1**.
4.  **Random Spin Sequence**:
    *   Inside the "if" block:
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [spinTime] to`**. From **Logic & Math**, drag **`random integer from [1] to [100]`** and set range to **1** to **4**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Snap variable **`spinTime`** into the duration field.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **LOW (0)**.
        *   **Debounce/Wait Release**: From **Logic & Math**, drag **`repeat while`**.
        *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btn`** equals **1**:
            *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.01** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
You press the button. The wheel spins. It stops after a random time, pointing to a random slice of the pie.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time
import random

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
motor = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    if btn.value():
        # Choose a random length for the turn
        spinTime = random.uniform(1.0, 4.0)
        
        motor.value(1) # Start spinning
        time.sleep(spinTime)
        motor.value(0) # Stop
        
        # Prevent rapid re-triggers
        while btn.value():
            time.sleep(0.01)
            
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Friction and Inertia**: Standard DC motors don't stop instantly. The momentum will keep the wheel coasting for a moment. This makes the game more interesting but less "precise" than an electronic randomizer.
*   **Integer vs Float**: If you use `math_random_int` (1, 4), the spin will always be exactly 1, 2, 3, or 4 seconds. For a smoother feel, a float (random.uniform) is better.
*   **Trigger Bounce**: If you forget to wait for the button release, the spin might trigger again immediately if your press was longer than the spin duration.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Electronic Braking**: If your driver supports it, set the motor pins to HIGH-HIGH at the end of `spinTime` to stop the wheel "dead" on a slice.
*   **Sound Effects**: Add a buzzer that "clicks" (Project 0021) faster or slower depending on the motor's power state.
*   **Winning Light**: Add an LED that flashes 3 times whenever the wheel stops to celebrate the result.

## Project 0039: Automated Motor (Smart Cooling)

### 2. Learning Objective
Closed-loop control (Bang-Bang). Turn a fan on if temperature exceeds a limit.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Thermostat Logic**: Basic feedback loop.
*   **Hysteresis**: Preventing rapid on/off cycling.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Temp Sensor (Internal)**
*   **DC Motor + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Temp Sensor** | ADC(4) | RP2040 Internal Temperature |
| **Driver IN-A** | GP14 | Fan Control Signal |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power for Fan |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] > [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`pico_read_internal_temp`** (read sensor)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **temp**: Number variable used to store the converted temperature in Celsius.
*   **motor**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` to drive the cooling fan.
*   **sensor**: ADC object initialized on channel 4 (Internal Sensor).

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`motor`**, **`sensor`**, and **`temp`**.
    *   Set **`motor`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`sensor`** to **ADC(4)**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Read and Convert Temperature**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [temp] to`**. From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`pico_read_internal_temp`**.
4.  **Hysteresis Controller Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a multi-branch **`controls_if`** block. Use the gear icon to add an `else if` branch.
    *   **Hot Branch**: If **`temp`** > **25**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Cool Branch**: Else If **`temp`** < **24**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Sampling Rate**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** seconds. (Check temp every second).

### 9. Execution Flow
If it's hot (25+), fan runs. It stays running until it cools down to 24. This 1-degree gap prevents the fan from flickering on/off rapidly at 25.0 degrees.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Initialization
motor = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
sensor = ADC(4) # Internal temperature sensor
conversion_factor = 3.3 / (65535)

while True:
    # Math to convert raw ADC bits to Voltage then to Celsius
    reading = sensor.read_u16() * conversion_factor
    temp = 27 - (reading - 0.706)/0.001721
    
    # Hysteresis Logic: Prevents "chattering" at the threshold
    if temp > 25:
        motor.value(1) # Start Cooling
    elif temp < 24:
        motor.value(0) # Stop Cooling
        
    time.sleep(1.0)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **ADC Math Errors**: The internal temperature sensor on the RP2040 is not perfectly linear. If your readings are off by 5-10 degrees, double check the `conversion_factor` and the offset constant (0.706).
*   **Hysteresis Too Narrow**: If you set the ON threshold at 25 and the OFF at 24.9, the motor will "chatter" (turn on and off several times per second) as the temperature fluctuates naturally. Always keep a 1-2 degree gap.
*   **Sensor Noise**: Heat from the motor driver or the Pico's own CPU can skew internal temperature readings. For high precision, an external sensor (like DS18B20) is recommended.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Proportional Speed**: Instead of ON/OFF, use PWM (Project 0034) to make the fan spin faster as the temperature rises.
*   **Overheat Alarm**: Trigger a buzzer (Project 0021) if the temperature exceeds a "Critical" level (e.g., 40C).
*   **Data Logging**: Use the **Terminal** to print the temperature every 5 seconds so you can graph the cooling efficiency.

## Project 0040: Mastering Motors (Soft Start)

### 2. Learning Objective
Protect mechanical parts from stress. Ramp up speed slowly instead of jumping to 100%.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Ramping**: Changing value linearly over time.
*   **For Loops**: Iteration.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Motor + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Driver IN-A** | GP14 | PWM Soft-Start Input |
| **Driver VCC** | VBUS | 5V Power Supply |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`count with [i] from [0] to [65535] by [1000]`** (controls_for)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`** (pulse Pin ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **i**: Loop variable used to iterate through the increasing Duty Cycle values.
*   **motor**: PWM object initialized on GP14 for gradual acceleration.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`motor`** and **`i`**.
    *   Set **`motor`** to **`PWM(Pin(14))`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.
2.  **Configure Frequency**:
    *   Set **`motor`** frequency to **100Hz**.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Rest Period**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and Duty to **0**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
4.  **Ramp Up Sequence**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`count with [i] from [0] to [65535] by [1000]`** block.
    *   Inside the loop:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and Duty to variable **`i`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.05** seconds.
5.  **Steady State**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds (Run at max speed).
6.  **Safety Stop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **14** (motor) and Duty to **0**.

### 9. Execution Flow
The motor whines, then slowly starts rotating, picking up speed over 3 seconds until it hits max RPM. This mimics how elevators and trains start smoothly.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Initialization
motor = PWM(Pin(14))
motor.freq(100)

while True:
    motor.duty_u16(0) # Ensure stop
    time.sleep(2)
    
    # Ramp Up: Gradeally increase power from 0% to 100%
    for i in range(0, 65536, 1000):
        motor.duty_u16(i)
        time.sleep(0.05)
        
    time.sleep(2) # Hold Top Speed
    motor.duty_u16(0) # Hard Stop
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Step Size Too Large**: If the step size is e.g. 30000, the "ramp" will feel more like two jerky jumps rather than a smooth acceleration.
*   **Too Fast Updates**: If the sleep inside the loop is too small (e.g. 0.001s), the motor will ramp up so fast it feels like an instant start.
*   **Lack of Frequency**: PWM frequency is critical for Soft Start. If the frequency is too high, some motor drivers might react poorly to low duty cycles. Ensure `motor.freq(100)` is set.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Soft Stop**: Create a second for-loop that ramps the speed back down from 65535 to 0.
*   **Exponential Ramp**: Change the math so that the motor accelerates slowly at first and then very quickly (Squaring the loop index).
*   **Load Test**: Add a physical load (like a heavy paper fan) to see how the Soft Start reduces the "kick" when the motor starts.

#  Batch 5: Traffic Lights 1

## 1. Project 0041: Introduction to Traffic Lights

### 2. Learning Objective
Control a complex multi-LED module. You will replicate a standard traffic signal cycle (Green-Yellow-Red), implementing safety delays critical for real-world intersections.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Cycling**: Fixed order of operations.
*   **Safety Critical Systems**: Understanding why timing matters (Red overlap).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Red, Yellow, Green LEDs (Traffic Light Module)**


### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | High Voltage Signal |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | Caution Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | Clear to Proceed Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **red**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(13, Pin.OUT)` for the stop signal.
*   **yel**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` for the caution signal.
*   **grn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)` for the go signal.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`red`**, **`yel`**, and **`grn`**.
    *   Set **`red`** to **`Pin(13, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`yel`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`grn`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Green Light Phase (GO)**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **1**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`red`** to **0**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds.
4.  **Yellow Light Phase (CAUTION)**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **1**, and **`red`** to **0**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
5.  **Red Light Phase (STOP)**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`red`** to **1**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
The standard American sequence: Cars go (Green), Cars caution (Yellow), Cars stop (Red). The system loops forever.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # State: Green (Go)
    red.value(0)
    yel.value(0)
    grn.value(1)
    time.sleep(5)
    
    # State: Yellow (Caution)
    grn.value(0)
    yel.value(1)
    time.sleep(2)
    
    # State: Red (Stop)
    yel.value(0)
    red.value(1)
    time.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **GPIO Conflict (Overlap)**: Forgetting to set the previous light to LOW when turning on the next one. This creates a confusing signal where two colors are on simultaneously.
*   **Missing Safety Delay**: In real systems, there is often a short "All Red" period where all directions are blocked to account for late-crossers. Omitting this can be dangerous.
*   **PWM Misuse**: Using PWM to control traffic LEDs is unnecessary and can cause flickering. Simple `Digital Output` (set to 0 or 1) is the standard for signaling.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Rush Hour Mode**: Change the timing so that Green lasts 10 seconds while Red only lasts 3 seconds.
*   **Night Mode**: After 5 cycles, make the Yellow LED blink (Project 0042) for a few seconds to simulate an empty junction.
*   **Walk Signal**: Add a 4th LED (White/Blue) that turns on only when the Red light is active to indicate it's safe for pedestrians.

## 1. Project 0042: Blinking Traffic Lights

### 2. Learning Objective
Simulate failure modes or special states. Flashing Yellow indicates "Proceed with Caution".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Indication**: Using blink patterns to communicate status.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Traffic Light Module (Yellow)**


### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | Maintenance Alert Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **yel**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)` used for the maintenance blink signal.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Selection Port**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_setup_pin`**.
    *   Select **GP14** and set to **Output**.
2.  **Assign to Variable**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`variables_set`**. Create variable **`yel`** and snap the setup block inside.
3.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Blink Sequence**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (yel) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.5** seconds.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (yel) and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.5** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Maintenance Power**: The system enters maintenance mode.
2.  **Cycle**: The Yellow LED turns ON for 0.5s, then OFF for 0.5s.
3.  **Repeat**: This creates a persistent caution signal.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    yel.value(1) # ON
    time.sleep(0.5)
    yel.value(0) # OFF
    time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Inconsistent Timing**: Using different `wait` times for ON and OFF states. This makes the signal look like a "fault" rather than a professional maintenance strobe.
*   **Peripheral Ghosting**: Leaving the other LEDs (Red/Green) powered on while blinking yellow. Always ensure unused pins are set to LOW.
*   **Loop Stuck**: If you add too much code inside the loop without appropriate spacing, the blinking timing might become erratic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Double Flash**: Blink twice quickly (0.1s each) followed by a 1-second pause to simulate a modern emergency strobe.
*   **Dual Maintenance**: Blink both Red and Yellow alternately for a high-priority "Road Closed" alert.
*   **Brightness Control**: Use PWM (Project 0016) to make the Yellow light "breathe" (fade in and out) instead of a hard blink.

## Project 0043: Manual Traffic Lights Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Interrupt a cycle for manual control. A "Police" button forces all lights Red immediately.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Priority Logic**: Safety overrides convenience.
*   **Polling**: Checking sensor state in high-frequency samples.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Traffic Light Module (Red, Yellow, Green)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Police Override Switch (Pull-Down) |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | High Alert Signal |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | Preparation Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | Normal Traffic Flow |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic, drag `controls_if`** (if / else)
*   **from Functions, drag `procedures_defnoreturn`** (define function)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for the manual switch.
*   **red**, **yel**, **grn**: Pin objects initialized as `Pin(X, Pin.OUT)` to control the signals.
*   **r, y, g**: Arguments for the `set_lights` function.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Interface Pins**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_setup_pin`**.
    *   Select **GP10** (Input/PullDown) -> assign to variable **btn** (from **Variables**).
    *   Select **GP13** (Output) -> assign to variable **red**.
    *   Select **GP14** (Output) -> assign to variable **yel**.
    *   Select **GP15** (Output) -> assign to variable **grn**.
2.  **Define Utility Function**:
    *   From **Functions**, define **`set_lights`** with arguments `r, y, g`.
    *   Inside: Set **red** to `r`, **yel** to `y`, and **grn** to `g`.
3.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
4.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
5.  **Control Logic**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with `else`).
    *   **Override Branch**: If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** is **1**:
        *   Then: Call **`set_lights(1, 0, 0)`**.
        *   Wait **0.1s**.
    *   **Normal Cycle Branch (Else)**:
        *   Follow standard sequence (G 5s -> Y 2s -> R 5s).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Normal**: The lights cycle as usual.
2.  **Override**: A police officer holds the button, forcing Red immediately.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

def set_lights(r, y, g):
    red.value(r); yel.value(y); grn.value(g)

while True:
    if btn.value():
        set_lights(1, 0, 0)
        time.sleep(0.1)
    else:
        set_lights(0, 0, 1); time.sleep(5)
        set_lights(0, 1, 0); time.sleep(2)
        set_lights(1, 0, 0); time.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **The Wait Trap**: Sleep blocks the button. Use polling for better response.
*   **Floating Pins**: Ensure PullDown is enabled.
*   **Light Sync**: Always update all three LEDs to avoid forbidden states.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Strobe Warning**: Pulse the Red light during override.
*   **LCD Status**: Print "POLICE OVERRIDE" to the terminal.
*   **Buzzer Alert**: Chirp the buzzer during the manual phase.

## Project 0044: Traffic Lights Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Regional coding. The UK uses a Red + Yellow phase to signal preparation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Compound States**: Driving multiple pins High at once.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Traffic Light Module**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | UK Red Phase |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | UK Preparatory Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | UK Flow Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **red**, **yel**, **grn**: Pin objects initialized as `Pin(X, Pin.OUT)` to control signals.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP13** (Output) -> `red`.
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Output) -> `yel`.
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `grn`.
2.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** into **`start`**.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
4.  **UK Cycle Phases**:
    *   **STOP Phase**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`red`** to **1**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`grn`** to **0**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **3.0** seconds.
    *   **PREPARE Phase**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **`yel`** to **1** (Red remains 1). From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** second.
    *   **GO Phase**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`red`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`grn`** to **1**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds.
    *   **CAUTION Phase**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`red`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **1**, and **`grn`** to **0**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
Red -> (Red+Yellow) -> Green -> Yellow -> Red.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # STOP
    red.value(1); yel.value(0); grn.value(0); time.sleep(3)
    # PREPARE
    yel.value(1); time.sleep(1)
    # GO
    red.value(0); yel.value(0); grn.value(1); time.sleep(5)
    # CAUTION
    grn.value(0); yel.value(1); time.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Flipping Phases**: Pedestrians and cars depend on predictable light patterns.
*   **Sticking Pins**: Ensure every phase sets all 3 pins to avoid mixed colors.
*   **UK Standard**: Don't turn OFF Red when Yellow turns ON for the preparatory phase.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dynamic Pulse**: Slow down the cycle at night (requires Project 0046 logic).
*   **Double Red**: Use two traffic modules facing opposite ways.
*   **Siren Sync**: Sound a buzzer during specific phases.

## Project 0045: Interactive Traffic Lights

### 2. Learning Objective
Actuated control. The light remains Green for cars until a button request is received.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Locking**: Keeping a state indefinitely.
*   **Event Triggering**: Transitioning only on user input.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Traffic Light Module**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Walk Button** | GP10 | Crossing Request (Pull-Down) |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | STOP Signal |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | CAUTION Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | GO Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`repeat while`** (controls_whileUntil)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` to detect requests.
*   **red**, **yel**, **grn**: Output Pin objects.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Input/Output**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP10** (Input/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    *   Setup **GP13, 14, 15** (Outputs) -> `red, yel, grn`.
2.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** into **`start`**.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
4.  **Idle Logic**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`red`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`grn`** to **1**.
5.  **Wait for Walker**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`repeat while`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btn`** equals **0**.
    *   **Inside**: (Empty, the code just waits here).
6.  **Transition Sequence**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds (Safety window).
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **1**, and **`red`** to **0**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`red`** to **1**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds (Pedestrian Walk phase).
7.  **Reset**:
    *   The loop returns to the Start to set the light back to Green.

### 9. Execution Flow
Green (Forever) -> Button Press -> Yellow -> Red (Pedestrians Walk) -> Green.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT); yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT); grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    red.value(0); yel.value(0); grn.value(1)
    while not btn.value():
        time.sleep(0.1)
    
    time.sleep(2)
    grn.value(0); yel.value(1); time.sleep(2)
    yel.value(0); red.value(1); time.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Polling Frequency**: A slow check might miss the button.
*   **Priority Flip**: Ensuring cars get Green back after the person is done.
*   **Safety Buffer**: Always wait a few seconds after a button press before changing the light.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Flash-Wait**: Flash a blue LED when the request is registered.
*   **Dual-Crossing**: Two buttons, one light.
*   **Acoustic Signal**: Beep the buzzer during the Walk phase.

## Project 0046: Smart Traffic Lights Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Adaptive traffic control. Use an LDR to switch between "Standard Cycle" (Day) and "Caution Blink" (Night).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Thresholding**: Using analog values to trigger mode changes.
*   **Mode Switching**: Executing different code paths based on environment.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LDR (Light Sensor)**
*   **Traffic Light Module (Red, Yellow, Green LEDs)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR Sensor** | GP26 (ADC0) | Ambient Brightness Input |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | Stop Signal |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | Caution Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | Go Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] > [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`** (pico_sensor_read)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_toggle`** (toggle Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **ldr**: ADC object initialized as `ADC(Pin(26))` to sense light levels.
*   **red**, **yel**, **grn**: Pin objects configured as `Pin(X, Pin.OUT)` for signal control.
*   **light**: Number variable created in **Variables** to store the 16-bit ADC sample.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Sensors**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_setup_pin`**. Select **GP26** and set to **ADC**.
    *   From **Variables**, create variable **ldr** and assign the setup to it.
2.  **Configure Outputs**:
    *   Setup **GP13** (Output) -> assign to variable **red**.
    *   Setup **GP14** (Output) -> assign to variable **yel**.
    *   Setup **GP15** (Output) -> assign to variable **grn**.
3.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** all setup blocks into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
4.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
5.  **Environment Check**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [light] to`**. From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`** for **ldr**.
6.  **Operational Mode Branch**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with `else`).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`light`** > **30000** (Environment is Bright/Day).
    *   **Standard Cycle (If Branch)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`red`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`grn`** to **1**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **3.0** seconds.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **1**, and **`red`** to **0**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** second.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** three times. Set **`grn`** to **0**, **`yel`** to **0**, and **`red`** to **1**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **3.0** seconds.
    *   **Caution Mode (Else Branch)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** for **`red`** to **0** and **`grn`** to **0**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_toggle`** for **`yel`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.5** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Sense**: Every 0.5s or after a cycle, the Pico checks the LDR.
2.  **Day**: If bright, the lights function as a regular traffic signal.
3.  **Night**: If dark, the system enters "Caution" mode, blinking the yellow light indefinitely.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import ADC, Pin
import time

# Initialization
ldr = ADC(Pin(26))
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    light = ldr.read_u16()
    
    if light > 30000: 
        # DAY MODE: Standard Sequence
        red.value(0); yel.value(0); grn.value(1)
        time.sleep(3)
        grn.value(0); yel.value(1)
        time.sleep(1)
        yel.value(0); red.value(1)
        time.sleep(3)
    else: 
        # NIGHT MODE: Caution Blink
        red.value(0); grn.value(0)
        yel.toggle() 
        time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Shadow Jitter**: A passing cloud might make the light "Night Mode" for a few seconds. This is why professional systems use "Averaging" or longer delays before switching modes.
*   **Feedback**: Ensure the traffic LEDs aren't pointing at the LDR, or the Green light might trick the sensor into thinking it's Morning.
*   **Inverted Logic**: Depending on your LDR divider, 65535 might be Dark or Bright. Print the value to verify.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Threshold**: Add a Potentiometer (Project 0025) to adjust when the light switches modes.
*   **Sunset Fade**: Use PWM (Project 0016) to make the Green light dimmer during the transition.
*   **Status Printer**: Print "MODE: DAY" or "MODE: NIGHT" to the console whenever the state changes.

## Project 0047: Traffic Lights Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Enforce traffic laws. Trigger a high-speed camera strobe if a car crosses the intersection during a Red light.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Event Handling**: Monitoring a sensor only during specific logic phases.
*   **Visual Alert**: Rapid strobe patterns (staccato blink).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button (Car Sensor)**
*   **Traffic Light Module (Red LED)**
*   **White LED (Camera Strobe)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button (Sensor)** | GP10 | Car Pressure Simulation (Pull-Down) |
| **Camera LED** | GP16 | Simulation Flash (White LED) |
| **Red LED** | GP13 | Junction Stop Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`repeat [3] times`** (controls_repeat_ext)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Functions**, drag **`define [function name]`** (procedures_defnoreturn)
*   From **Functions**, drag the call block for the function.
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for car detection.
*   **cam**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(16, Pin.OUT)` to control the camera strobe.
*   **red**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(13, Pin.OUT)` for the stop signal.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Interface Pins**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP10** (Input/PullDown) -> assign to variable **btn** (from **Variables**).
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP16** (Output) -> assign to variable **cam**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP13** (Output) -> assign to variable **red**.
2.  **Define Violation Procedure**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag **`procedures_defnoreturn`**. Name it **`check_violation`**.
    *   Inside: From **Logic**, drag **`controls_if`**.
    *   **Condition**: If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** is **1**:
        *   From **Loops**, drag **`controls_repeat_ext`** (3 times).
        *   Inside: Set **cam** to 1, wait 0.05s, set **cam** to 0, wait 0.05s.
3.  **Finalize Setup**:
    *   **Snap** all setup blocks into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
4.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
5.  **Enforcement Cycle (RED Phase)**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **`red`** to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`repeat [50] times`** block.
    *   Inside the loop:
        *   From **Functions**, drag the call block for function **`check_violation`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.1** seconds. (Total: 5s enforcement).
6.  **Clear Phase (GREEN)**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **`red`** to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds. (Car detection is ignored during this phase).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Safety**: While light is Red, the system samples the car sensor 10 times per second.
2.  **Trigger**: If the sensor goes High during Red, the white LED flashes 3 times rapidly.
3.  **Reset**: After Red ends, the light goes off (simulated Green) and the "flash trap" is inactive.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
cam = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)

def check_violation():
    if btn.value():
        # Triple-Strobe Alert
        for _ in range(3):
            cam.value(1); time.sleep(0.05)
            cam.value(0); time.sleep(0.05)

while True:
    # RED PHASE: Active Monitoring
    red.value(1)
    for _ in range(50):
        check_violation()
        time.sleep(0.1)
    
    # GREEN PHASE: Monitoring Disabled
    red.value(0)
    time.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Non-Atomic Monitoring**: If you use a single `wait(5.0s)` for the Red light, you won't be able to check the button during that time. You must use the "Repeat Loop + Short Wait" pattern.
*   **Strobe Feedback**: If the White LED is too close to the sensor (if using an LDR car sensor), it could re-trigger the violation check.
*   **Missing Grounds**: Car sensors often require a common ground with the Pico to avoid "Ghost" detections from static noise.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Evidence Logger**: Print "VIOLATION AT: [TICKS_MS]" to the console whenever the camera flashes.
*   **Yellow Grace Period**: Add a 1-second phase before Red where the camera is OFF to allow slow cars to clear.
*   **Multiple Lanes**: Add a second button (GP11) for Lane B and have the camera "Flash" twice for Lane A and once for Lane B.

## Project 0048: The Traffic Lights Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Event timing. Create a "Reaction Test" where the user must press a button as soon as the light turns Green.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Time Measurement**: Using `ticks_ms` to calculate duration.
*   **Randomization**: Adding unpredictability to the wait time.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **Traffic Light Module (Red, Yellow, Green)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Player Button** | GP10 | Reaction Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Yellow LED** | GP14 | Preparation Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | Start Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`repeat while`** (controls_whileUntil)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`get current time (ms)`** (pico_ticks_ms)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`random integer from [1] to [100]`** (math_random_int)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` to detect the player's reaction.
*   **start_time**: Number variable used to store the timestamp when the light turns green.
*   **reaction_time**: Number variable used to store the calculated duration (ms).

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`start_time`**, and **`reaction_time`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Game Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **The Wait**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (yellow) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Snap **`random integer from [1] to [5]`** from **Logic & Math** into the duration field.
4.  **GO Signal**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **14** (yellow) to **LOW (0)** and Pin to **15** (green) to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [start_time] to`**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`get current time (ms)`**.
5.  **Wait for Response**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`repeat while`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **`btn`** equals **0**.
    *   **Inside**: (Empty).
6.  **Calculate Results**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [reaction_time] to`**.
    *   Subtract **`start_time`** from **`get current time (ms)`** using **Logic & Math** operators.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_log`**. Create text with "Reaction: " and the **`reaction_time`** variable.
7.  **Reset Game**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (green) to **LOW (0)** and wait **3.0** seconds before restarting.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Ready**: Yellow light illuminates (Get Ready signal).
2.  **Random Wait**: System waits 1-5 seconds randomly to prevent anticipation.
3.  **GO**: Green light turns on, timestamp captured.
4.  **Player Response**: User presses button as fast as possible.
5.  **Calculate**: Reaction time = (end_time - start_time) displayed in milliseconds.
6.  **Reset**: Green off, pause 3 seconds, restart game.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time
import random

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
yel = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Get Ready Phase
    yel.value(1)
    time.sleep(random.randint(1, 5))
    
    # GO Signal
    yel.value(0)
    grn.value(1)
    start_time = time.ticks_ms()
    
    # Wait for Player
    while not btn.value():
        pass
    
    # Calculate Reaction Time
    reaction_time = time.ticks_diff(time.ticks_ms(), start_time)
    print(f"Reaction: {reaction_time} ms")
    
    # Reset
    grn.value(0)
    time.sleep(3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Anticipation**: Users might hold the button. Add logic to check if the button is ALREADY pressed when the light turns Green.
*   **Math Order**: Always subtract Start from End, not the other way around.
*   **Ticks vs Seconds**: Ticks are milliseconds. Divide by 1000 for seconds.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **False Start**: If the button is pressed during the Red phase, turn OFF the game and flash the light.
*   **Best Time**: Store the fastest reaction in a variable and display it.
*   **Sound Starter**: Use a buzzer to "Chirp" when Green turns on.

## Project 0049: Automated Traffic Lights

### 2. Learning Objective
Presence detection. Trigger the light only when a car is physically present.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Pulse Echo**: Measuring distance via sound waves.
*   **Wait until Condition**: Pausing execution for sensors.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Ultrasonic Sensor (HC-SR04)**
*   **Traffic Light Module**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Trigger** | GP16 | Ping Signal (Output) |
| **Echo** | GP17 | Return Signal (Input) |
| **Traffic Module** | GP13, 14, 15 | Car Signals |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] < [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_ultrasonic_dist`** (get distance cm)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **dist**: Number variable created in **Variables** to store the measurement.
*   **trig**, **echo**: Pin identifiers for the ultrasonic sensor setup.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variable **`dist`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Idle State**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **13** (red) to **HIGH (1)** and Pin to **15** (green) to **LOW (0)**.
4.  **Distance Check**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [dist] to`**. From **Smart IO**, drag **`get distance cm`**. Set Trig to **16** and Echo to **17**.
5.  **Trigger Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with `else`).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`dist`** < **10**.
    *   **Action (If)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **red** to **LOW (0)** and **green** to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds.
    *   **Action (Else)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **red** to **HIGH (1)** and **green** to **LOW (0)**.
6.  **Loop Stability**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.1** seconds.

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **The Cone Effect**: Ultrasonic sensors have a 15-degree cone. Objects to the side might trigger it.
*   **Soft Objects**: Clothes and curtains absorb sound; use hard-surfaced targets for testing.
*   **Rapid Pings**: Don't ping more than 20 times per second to avoid echo overlap.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Approach Warning**: Blink Yellow faster as the car gets closer.
*   **Tailgate Alarm**: Sound a buzzer if a car is within 5cm of another car's sensor.
*   **LCD Distance**: Display the car's distance in cm on a screen.

## Project 0050: Mastering Traffic Lights

### 2. Learning Objective
Manage two intersecting flows. 2 Traffic lights must be perfectly synced.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Synchronization**: Shared state.
*   **Deadlock Prevention**.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2x Traffic Light Modules (6 LEDs total)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **North Red** | GP10 | Road A Stop Signal |
| **North Yellow** | GP11 | Road A Caution Signal |
| **North Green** | GP12 | Road A Go Signal |
| **West Red** | GP13 | Road B Stop Signal |
| **West Yellow** | GP14 | Road B Caution Signal |
| **West Green** | GP15 | Road B Go Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Functions**, drag **`define [function name]`** (procedures_defnoreturn)
*   From **Functions**, drag the call block for the function.
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **nR, nY, nG**: North direction Pins.
*   **wR, wY, wG**: West direction Pins.
*   **nr, ny, ng, wr, wy, wg**: Arguments for the synchronized state function.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables for all 6 LEDs (**`nR`** to **`wG`**).
    *   Set each to its respective **Pin(X, Pin.OUT)**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.
2.  **Define Sync Function**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag the **`define [function name]`** block. Name it **`set_traffic`**. Add 6 arguments: **`nr, ny, ng, wr, wy, wg`**.
    *   Inside the function: 
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** 6 times.
        *   Set **nR** to variable **`nr`**, **nY** to variable **`ny`**, etc.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
4.  **Flow A (North-South Go)**:
    *   From **Functions**, drag the call block for **`set_traffic`**.
    *   Set arguments: **0, 0, 1, 1, 0, 0** (North Green, West Red).
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **5.0** seconds.
5.  **Transition A (North-South Caution)**:
    *   Call **`set_traffic`**: **0, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0** (North Yellow).
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds.
6.  **Flow B (East-West Go)**:
    *   Call **`set_traffic`**: **1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1** (West Green).
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **5.0** seconds.
7.  **Transition B (East-West Caution)**:
    *   Call **`set_traffic`**: **1, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0** (West Yellow).
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Phase 1**: North flows (Green), West waits (Red).
2.  **Phase 2**: North stops (Yellow -> Red).
3.  **Phase 3**: Safety Gap (Both Red).
4.  **Phase 4**: West flows (Green), North waits (Red).
5.  **Phase 5**: West stops (Yellow -> Red).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
nR = Pin(10, Pin.OUT)
nY = Pin(11, Pin.OUT)
nG = Pin(12, Pin.OUT)
wR = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
wY = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
wG = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

# Synchronized Light Update
def set_traffic(nr, ny, ng, wr, wy, wg):
    nR.value(nr); nY.value(ny); nG.value(ng)
    wR.value(wr); wY.value(wy); wG.value(wg)

while True:
    # North Road Flows
    set_traffic(0, 0, 1, 1, 0, 0)
    time.sleep(5)
    
    # North Road Stops
    set_traffic(0, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0)
    time.sleep(2)
    
    # Safety Transition (All Red)
    set_traffic(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # West Road Flows
    set_traffic(1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)
    time.sleep(5)
    
    # West Road Stops
    set_traffic(1, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0)
    time.sleep(2)
    
    # Safety Transition (All Red)
    set_traffic(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0)
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Logical Deadlock (Both Red Forever)**: If you accidentally call `set_traffic(1,0,0,1,0,0)` without any subsequent GO phases, the intersection will be permanently blocked.
*   **Conflicting Greens**: The most dangerous mistake is having both `nG` and `wG` set to 1. Using a single `set_traffic` function as a "Source of Truth" makes this much harder to do by accident.
*   **Missing Buffer**: Swapping Green directly from North to West without a Yellow/Red pause will cause cars already in the junction to collide with new traffic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dynamic Weighting**: If a button (Project 0045) is pressed for the West road, make the North road turn Red immediately for better response.
*   **Emergency Mode**: Add a manual override (Project 0043) that turns ALL lights to flashing Yellow.
*   **LED Logic Check**: Add a small diagnostic check at the start of the code that turns on every LED one by one for 0.5s to ensure no bulbs are blown.

## Project 0051: Introduction to Night Light

### 2. Learning Objective
Create a conceptual night light. Simply turn on the LED and keep it on. This represents the "Safety" state of a night light.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Continuous Operation**: Systems that run forever.
*   **Power State**: Determining On vs Off.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED** | GP15 | Safety Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure LED Variable**:
    *   Create variable **`led`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
    *   **Snap** below the initialization section.
3.  **Turn On**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **1.0** second (Keep CPU happy).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Power Up**: The Pico starts.
2.  **Action**: It turns GP15 High (3.3V).
3.  **State**: The LED emits light.
4.  **Loop**: It does nothing else but keep the light on.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    led.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Pin**: If you plugged the LED into GP14 but code says GP15, it won't light up.
*   **No Resistor**: Without a resistor, the LED might burn out or draw too much current.
*   **Inverted LED**: LEDs are polar. If it doesn't light, try flipping the long and short legs.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Add Switch**: See Project 0053 to control the light with a button.
*   **Brightness**: Change it to 50% brightness (requires PWM, see Project 0016).
*   **Color Pulse**: If using an RGB LED, cycle through colors instead of just white.

## Project 0052: Blinking Night Light

### 2. Learning Objective
Pulses the light slowly (Fade In/Out) to mimic breathing, aiding relaxation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **PWM Fading**: Varying brightness smoothly.
*   **Nested Loops**: Incrementing up, then decrementing down.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **PWM-capable LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED** | GP15 | Pulsing Sleep Aid |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`count with [i] from [0] to [65535] by [1000]`** (controls_for)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`** (pulse Pin ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **led**: PWM object initialized as `PWM(Pin(15))` with a frequency of `1000Hz`.
*   **i**: Loop variable used to iterate through duty cycle values.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure PWM Variable**:
    *   Create variable **`led`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`PWM(Pin(15))`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.
2.  **Set Frequency**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **led**, Frequency to **1000**, and Duty to **0**.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
4.  **Transition: Fade In**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag the **`count with [i] from [0] to [65535] by [1000]`** block.
    *   Inside: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **led**, Frequency to **1000**, and Duty to **i**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.05** seconds.
5.  **Transition: Fade Out**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag another **`count`** block.
    *   Set range from **65535** down to **0** by **-1000**.
    *   Inside: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **led**, Frequency to **1000**, and Duty to **i**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.05** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Breath In**: The light slowly brightens from 0 to 100% over several seconds.
2.  **Peak**: It reaches full brightness.
3.  **Breath Out**: The light slowly dims from 100% back to 0.
4.  **Repeat**: Creates a calming, rhythmic effect.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Initialization
led = PWM(Pin(15))
led.freq(1000)

while True:
    # Fade In
    for i in range(0, 65536, 1000):
        led.duty_u16(i)
        time.sleep(0.05)
        
    # Fade Out
    for i in range(65535, -1, -1000):
        led.duty_u16(i)
        time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Frequency Flicker**: If led.freq() is not set (or too low), the fading will flicker visibly. 1000Hz is recommended.
*   **Step Size**: Using a step of 1 instead of 1000 will make the fade take several minutes to complete.
*   **Limit Handling**: The `range` function is exclusive. Ensure your loop includes the full 0-65535 range.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Heartbeat**: Modify the sleep times to create a fast double-pulse "heartbeat" effect.
*   **Random Pulse**: Use a random integer to pick the peak brightness of each "breath".
*   **Multi-LED Breath**: Add a second LED on GP14 and make it fade out while the first fades in.

## Project 0053: Manual Night Light Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Add a switch. Manually toggle the night light mode.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Detection**: Reading digital inputs from a switch.
*   **Conditional Logic**: IF/ELSE branches for power states.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Slide Switch or Push Button**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Switch** | GP10 | ON/OFF Selector (Pull-Down) |
| **LED** | GP15 | Main Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **sw**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`sw`** and **`led`**.
    *   Set **`sw`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **State Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (click the gear to add an **else** branch).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **sw** equals **1**.
    *   **Action (If - Switch is ON)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - Switch is OFF)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
4.  **Loop Stability**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds to prevent high CPU usage.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: You slide the switch to ON.
2.  **Logic**: The Pico reads "High".
3.  **Output**: It turns the LED "High".
4.  **Input**: You slide back to OFF.
5.  **Output**: It turns the LED "Low".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
sw = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    if sw.value() == 1:
        led.value(1)
    else:
        led.value(0)
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Floating Pins**: If you forget `Pin.PULL_DOWN`, the pin might read random electrical noise and flicker the light.
*   **Button vs Switch**: A button only stays on while held. A slide switch physically stays in place. The code works for both, but the user experience differs.
*   **Wait Timing**: Too long of a wait (e.g., 5s) will make the switch feel unresponsive.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Reverse Logic**: Modify the code so the light turns ON when the switch is LOW.
*   **Diagnostic LED**: Add a second LED to GP14 that turns ON when the main light is OFF.
*   **Log State**: Use `pico_log` to print "Night Light: ACTIVE" or "Night Light: STANDBY" to the console.
