## Project 0054: Night Light Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Color cycling. Slowly transition Red -> Green -> Blue to create different room atmospheres.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **RGB Color Mixing**: Combining primary colors.
*   **Sequence Timing**: Maintaining a color state for a set duration.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x RGB LED (Common Cathode recommended)**
*   **3x 220 Ohm Resistors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Red Pin** | GP13 | Red Color Channel |
| **Green Pin** | GP14 | Green Color Channel |
| **Blue Pin** | GP15 | Blue Color Channel |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Cathode connection |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **red**, **grn**, **blu**: Pin objects initialized as `Pin(X, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`red`**, **`grn`**, and **`blu`**.
    *   Set **`red`** to **`Pin(13, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`grn`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`blu`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Color: Red**:
    *   Add three **`pico_gpio_write`** blocks.
    *   Set **red**=1, **grn**=0, **blu**=0.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds.
4.  **Color: Green**:
    *   Add three **`pico_gpio_write`** blocks.
    *   Set **red**=0, **grn**=1, **blu**=0.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds.
5.  **Color: Blue**:
    *   Add three **`pico_gpio_write`** blocks.
    *   Set **red**=0, **grn**=0, **blu**=1.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Red Phase**: The light glows Red for 2 seconds.
2.  **Green Phase**: It switches to Green for 2 seconds.
3.  **Blue Phase**: It switches to Blue for 2 seconds.
4.  **Loop**: Creating a calming, slow-moving color display.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
red = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
blu = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Red
    red.value(1); grn.value(0); blu.value(0)
    time.sleep(2)
    # Green
    red.value(0); grn.value(1); blu.value(0)
    time.sleep(2)
    # Blue
    red.value(0); grn.value(0); blu.value(1)
    time.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Common Anode LEDs**: If your LED uses "Common Anode", setting a pin High turns it OFF. You'll need to invert your 1s and 0s.
*   **Shorting Pins**: Always use resistors for each color pin (R, G, B) to prevent damage to the Pico.
*   **Pin Mismatch**: If the colors appear in the wrong order, swap the GP13, GP14, and GP15 wires.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Mixed Colors**: Try Cyan (Green+Blue=1) or Magenta (Red+Blue=1) phases.
*   **Variable Speed**: Use a variable for the wait time and change it with a button.
*   **Fading Moods**: Combine this with PWM (Project 0052) to fade between the colors smoothly.

## Project 0055: Interactive Night Light

### 2. Learning Objective
Audio trigger. Use a sound sensor to toggle the night light state with a loud noise like a clap.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Toggle Logic**: Changing a state from True to False (or 1 to 0).
*   **Debouncing (Anti-Echo)**: Preventing a single clap from triggering the light multiple times.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Digital Sound Sensor Module**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sound Sensor** | GP16 | Digital Output Pin |
| **LED** | GP15 | Night Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`not`** (logic_negate)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **mic**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(16, Pin.IN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **is_on**: Boolean variable used to track the light's current state.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`mic`**, **`led`**, and **`is_on`**.
    *   Set **`mic`** to **`Pin(16, Pin.IN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`is_on`** to **`false`** (from **Logic & Math**).
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Clap Detection**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **mic** equals **1**.
4.  **Toggling Action**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [is_on] to`**. From **Logic & Math**, drag **`not [is_on]`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **is_on**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.5** seconds (to avoid echo re-triggering).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Listen**: The system waits for a sound pulse from GP16.
2.  **Trigger**: A clap creates a high pulse.
3.  **Flip**: The `is_on` variable flips (e.g., from False to True).
4.  **Apply**: The LED state is updated to match `is_on`.
5.  **Cooldown**: Half a second delay ensures the sound of the light or room echo doesn't flip it back.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
mic = Pin(16, Pin.IN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
is_on = False

while True:
    if mic.value() == 1:
        # Flip the state
        is_on = not is_on
        led.value(is_on)
        # Prevent double triggering
        time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensitivity Screw**: Most sound sensors have a potentiometer (tiny screw). If it's too sensitive, background noise will toggle the light; if too low, even claps won't work.
*   **Missing Cooldown**: Without the 0.5s wait, a single long clap might toggle the light several dozen times, making it look random.
*   **State Conflict**: If you use `led.toggle()` without tracking `is_on`, the code is simpler but harder to expand with other logic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Sensitivity Print**: Use `pico_log` to see when the sensor triggers.
*   **Double Clap**: Change the logic so the light only toggles if TWO claps are detected within 1 second.
*   **LDR Safe**: Only allow the clap to work if the room is dark (combine with Project 0054).

## Project 0056: Smart Night Light Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Auto-brightness. Automatically turn the light on when it gets dark and off when it gets bright.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Analog Sensing**: Reading variable light levels (0-65535).
*   **Thresholding**: Deciding the exact point where "Dark" begins.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Light Dependent Resistor (LDR)**
*   **1x 10k Ohm Resistor (for Voltage Divider)**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR** | GP26 | Analog Input (ADC0) |
| **LED** | GP15 | Main Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] < [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`** (pico_sensor_read)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **ldr**: ADC object initialized as `ADC(26)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **light_level**: Number variable used to store the 16-bit analog reading.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`ldr`**, **`led`**, and **`light_level`**.
    *   Set **`ldr`** to **`ADC(26)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Read Sensor**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [light_level] to`**.
    *   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`**. Set Pin to **26**.
4.  **Automatic Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with **else**).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`light_level`** < **30000**.
    *   **Action (If - Dark)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - Bright)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Loop Stability**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Sense**: The Pico measures the voltage at GP26.
2.  **Compare**: The reading is compared to the 30,000 threshold.
3.  **Act**: If the value is low (indicating darkness/shadow), the LED turns on.
4.  **Wait**: A short delay prevents excessive polling.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Initialization
ldr = ADC(26)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Read raw 16-bit value
    light_level = ldr.read_u16()
    
    # Threshold check (Darkness < 30000)
    if light_level < 30000:
        led.value(1)
    else:
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wiring Inversion**: Depending on whether the LDR is connected to 3.3V or GND in the voltage divider, the reading might go UP or DOWN when it gets dark. Test and swap the `<` to `>` if needed.
*   **Feedback Loop**: If the LED is too close to the LDR, the LDR will think it's daytime as soon as the LED turns on, causing it to strobe rapidly. Distance them!
*   **Missing Threshold Adjustment**: 30,000 is a general starting point; your specific room lighting may require a different number (e.g., 10,000 or 50,000).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Hysteresis Logic**: Use two different thresholds (e.g., turn on at 20k, off at 40k) to prevent flickering at the "border" of darkness.
*   **Proportional Brightness**: Use PWM (Project 0052) instead of Digital Write to make the LED get brighter as the room gets darker.
*   **Daytime Override**: Add a button (Project 0053) to force the light off even if it's dark.

## Project 0057: Night Light Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Motion detection in the dark. Use an ultrasonic sensor to monitor for "monsters" (approaching objects) and trigger a warning light.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Proximity Alert**: Triggering actions based on distance thresholds.
*   **Digital Logic**: Combining sensor data with output states.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Ultrasonic Sensor (HC-SR04)**
*   **Red LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Trig** | GP16 | Trigger Pulse |
| **Echo** | GP17 | Distance measurement echo |
| **Red LED** | GP15 | Alarm Indicator |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] < [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`get distance cm`** (pico_ultrasonic_dist)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **dist**: Number variable used to store the calculated distance from the sensor.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variable **`dist`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Measure Proximity**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [dist] to`**.
    *   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`get distance cm`**. Set Trig to **16** and Echo to **17**.
4.  **Alarm Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with **else**).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`dist`** < **30**.
    *   **Action (If - Monster Detected)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (red) and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - Area Safe)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** (red) and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.2** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Listen**: The Pico sends an ultrasonic pulse and waits for the echo.
2.  **Evaluate**: If an object is detected within 30cm, the monster logic is triggered.
3.  **Signal**: The red LED lights up to warn the "monster" to stay away.
4.  **Loop**: The system continuously scans the area for threats.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
from pico_sensor import Ultrasonic
import time

# Initialization using standardized library helper
sensor = Ultrasonic(trig_pin=16, echo_pin=17)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Get distance in cm
    dist = sensor.distance_cm()
    
    # Check for proximity (< 30cm)
    if dist < 30:
        led.value(1)
    else:
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensor Blind Spots**: Objects too close (< 2cm) or too far (> 400cm) might cause erratic distance readings. 30cm is a safe middle-ground threshold.
*   **Incorrect Echo Pin**: If the Echo pin is connected to GP16 instead of GP17, the code will hang while waiting for pulses that never arrive.
*   **Power Stability**: Ultrasonic sensors drawing high current can cause the Pico's voltage to sag; ensure a stable 5V (USB) or 3.3V connection.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Audio Warning**: Add a buzzer (Project 0063) that beeps faster as the "monster" gets closer.
*   **Monster Counter**: Use `pico_log` to count and display how many times a monster was detected tonight.
*   **Panic Mode**: If the monster gets extremely close (< 10cm), flash the light rapidly instead of just turning it on.

## Project 0058: The Night Light Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Inverse logic as a game. Trigger the light to blink by casting a shadow over the sensor.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Shadow Detection**: Detecting a drop in light levels as an input event.
*   **Timed Feedback**: Using a temporary output signal to acknowledge an interaction.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Light Dependent Resistor (LDR)**
*   **1x 10k Ohm Resistor**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR** | GP26 | Light Intensity Sensor |
| **LED** | GP15 | Game Acknowledge Light |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] < [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`** (pico_sensor_read)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **ldr**: ADC object initialized as `ADC(26)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **light_level**: Number variable representing the current brightness.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`ldr`**, **`led`**, and **`light_level`**.
    *   Set **`ldr`** to **`ADC(26)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Monitor Shadow**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [light_level] to`**.
    *   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`**. Set Pin to **26**.
4.  **Acknowledgment Action**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`light_level`** < **10000** (Indicates a dense shadow).
    *   **Action (If - Shadow Detected)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: The Pico continuously checks the brightness at GP26.
2.  **Interact**: Wave your hand over the sensor.
3.  **Detect**: When the value falls below 10,000, the "shadow puppet" is recognized.
4.  **Confirm**: The LED blinks for 2 seconds to acknowledge the "puppet".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Initialization
ldr = ADC(26)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Read raw 16-bit value
    light_level = ldr.read_u16()
    
    # Shadow check (Deep shadow < 10000)
    if light_level < 10000:
        led.value(1)
        time.sleep(2) # Hold for 2s
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Ambient Light Calibration**: If the room is already dim, 10,000 might be too high (it triggers by itself). If the room is very bright, you might need a higher threshold.
*   **Quick Wave**: If you wave too fast, the 0.1s poll might miss the shadow. Try waving slower or decreasing the poll wait.
*   **Shadow Density**: Not all shadows are equal. Ensure you are blocking enough light to cross the threshold.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Shadow Piano**: Add multiple LDRs on different pins (GP26, GP27). Assign a different tone/LED color to each sensor to "play" the shadows.
*   **Double Shadow**: Only trigger if two shadows are detected within 3 seconds.
*   **Light Hunter**: Reverse the logic—trigger the light only when a flashlight is pointed directly at the LDR.

## Project 0059: Automated Night Light

### 2. Learning Objective
Timed event control. Create a light that stays on for a set duration after a button press, perfect for reading before sleep.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Timed Pulse**: Creating an output that lasts for a specific period (monostable logic).
*   **State Automation**: Automatically transitioning from ON to OFF without further user input.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Timer Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **LED** | GP15 | Night Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`** and **`led`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Check Trigger**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
4.  **Timed Action**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **15.0** seconds (Simulation of a longer timer).
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Polling Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Idle**: The Pico waits for a button press.
2.  **Trigger**: You press the button on GP10.
3.  **Active**: The LED turns on for exactly 15 seconds.
4.  **Reset**: The LED turns off automatically, and the code returns to the "Idle" state.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Check if button is pressed
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Turn light ON
        led.value(1)
        # Wait 15 seconds
        time.sleep(15)
        # Turn light OFF
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking Code**: This code "pauses" the Pico for 15 seconds. While the timer is running, the Pico cannot check for other buttons or sensors.
*   **Button Bounce**: A single press might trigger the timer twice if the button is "noisy." The 15s wait naturally "debounces" it by ignoring inputs while active.
*   **Duration Units**: Ensure you are using seconds (15) and not milliseconds (15000) or minutes, unless intended.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Extendable Timer**: Modify the code so that if the button is pressed *while* the light is on, the timer resets to 15 seconds again.
*   **Visual Warning**: Make the LED flash during the final 3 seconds to warn the user that the light is about to turn off.
*   **Multi-Stage Timer**: Press once for 15 seconds, twice for 30 seconds (requires a counter variable).

## Project 0060: Mastering Night Light

### 2. Learning Objective
Multifactor Logic (AND). Create an efficient system where the light only turns on if it is both Dark AND Motion is detected.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Boolean Logic (AND)**: Requiring two independent conditions to be True simultaneously.
*   **Power Efficiency**: Only activating high-current components (LEDs) when absolutely necessary.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Light Dependent Resistor (LDR)**
*   **1x PIR Motion Sensor**
*   **White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR** | GP26 | Ambient Light Sensor |
| **PIR Sensor** | GP16 | Motion Detector |
| **LED** | GP15 | Night Light Source |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`and`** (logic_operation)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] < [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`read analog sensor`** (pico_sensor_read)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **ldr**: ADC object initialized as `ADC(26)`.
*   **pir**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(16, Pin.IN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **light_level**: Number variable for brightness.
*   **motion**: Number variable for motion state.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`ldr`**, **`pir`**, **`led`**, **`light_level`**, and **`motion`**.
    *   Set **`ldr`** to **`ADC(26)`**.
    *   Set **`pir`** to **`Pin(16, Pin.IN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Read Inputs**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [light_level] to`**. From **Smart Sensors**, read Pin **26**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [motion] to`**. From **Smart IO**, read Pin **16**.
4.  **Combined Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (with **else**).
    *   **Condition**: Drag an **`and`** block.
        *   Left side: Check if **`light_level`** < **20000**.
        *   Right side: Check if **`motion`** equals **1**.
    *   **Action (If - Dark AND Motion)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - Bright OR Still)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Polling Frequency**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check Environment**: The Pico reads both the LDR and the PIR sensor.
2.  **Evaluate Boolean AND**:
    *   If the room is Bright, the light stays off even if you move.
    *   If the room is Dark but you stay still, the light stays off.
    *   If the room is Dark and you move, the light turns on.
3.  **Loop**: Continuous monitoring for efficient lighting.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Initialization
ldr = ADC(26)
pir = Pin(16, Pin.IN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Read sensor states
    light_level = ldr.read_u16()
    motion = pir.value()
    
    # Combined logic: Dark AND Motion
    if light_level < 20000 and motion == 1:
        led.value(1)
    else:
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensor Warm-up**: PIR sensors often need 30-60 seconds after power-on to calibrate. During this time, they may report "motion" constantly.
*   **Logic Swap**: If you use OR instead of AND, the light will turn on if it's dark (all night) or whenever you move (even in daytime).
*   **Threshold Mismatch**: Ensure the light from the LED doesn't hit the LDR, or the LDR will think it's light again and turn the LED off (flickering).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Hold Time**: Add a 10-second timer (Project 0059) so the light stays on for a while after motion stops.
*   **Warning Strobe**: If the PIR detects motion in the dark for more than 30 seconds, flash the LED to act as an intruder alert.
*   **Remote Log**: Use `pico_log` to send a message when someone enters the room at night.

## Project 0063: Manual Doorball Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Control pitch manually. Assign distinct tones to different buttons to identify which door a visitor is at.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Audio Distinction**: Using different frequencies to represent unique events.
*   **Conditional Branching**: Handling multiple inputs in a single loop.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2x Push Buttons**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Front Button** | GP10 | Main Entrance (Pull-Down) |
| **Back Button** | GP11 | Secondary Entrance (Pull-Down) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Passive Sound Output (PWM) |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic, drag `controls_if`** (if / else if / else)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm`** (pulse Pin ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn_front**, **btn_back**: Pin objects initialized as `Pin(X, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)` for input detection.
*   **buzzer**: PWM object initialized as `PWM(Pin(15))` to generate varied tones.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Input Buttons**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP10** (Input/PullDown) -> assign to variable **btn_front** (from **Variables**).
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP11** (Input/PullDown) -> assign to variable **btn_back**.
2.  **Configure Sound Device**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> assign to variable **buzzer**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
4.  **Priority logic**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag a **`controls_if`** block (add `else if` and `else` windows).
    *   **Case 1 (Front Door)**: If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn_front** is **1**:
        *   Then: From **Smart IO**, set **buzzer** (frequency) to **1000** Hz and (duty) to **50** %.
    *   **Case 2 (Back Door)**: If **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn_back** is **1**:
        *   Then: Set **buzzer** (frequency) to **500** Hz and (duty) to **50** %.
    *   **Case 3 (Idle)**: Otherwise (Else):
        *   Then: Set **buzzer** (duty) to **0** %.
5.  **Polling Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, wait **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Front press**: High pitch tone identifies the front entrance.
2.  **Back press**: Low pitch tone identifies the secondary entrance.
3.  **Silence**: Releasing the button stops the signal.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Initialization
btn_front = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_back = Pin(11, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = PWM(Pin(15))

while True:
    if btn_front.value():
        buzzer.freq(1000)
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
    elif btn_back.value():
        buzzer.freq(500)
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
    else:
        buzzer.duty_u16(0)
    
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Duty Cycle confusion**: 32768 is 50% volume. 65535 is basically silent (full high DC).
*   **Missing Duty Reset**: If you don't reset duty to 0 in the `else` block, the buzzer will hum forever.
*   **Frequency Range**: Tones below 100Hz might be too quiet for small buzzers; use 400Hz-2000Hz for best results.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Alert**: Flash a different colored LED for each door.
*   **Privacy Mode**: Add a third switch (GP12) that prevents any sound when activated.
*   **Alternating Tones**: Create a "Warble" by switching frequencies every 0.1s.

## Project 0064: Doorball Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Automate sound patterns. A single trigger initiates a multi-note sequence with precise timing.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sequential Tones**: Playing different frequencies in a specific order.
*   **Note Sustain**: Controlling the duration a sound is held.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Sequence Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Passive Sound Output |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`** (pulse Pin ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **buzzer**: PWM object initialized as `PWM(Pin(15))`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`** and **`buzzer`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`buzzer`** to **`PWM(Pin(15))`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Check Trigger**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
4.  **Chime Sequence**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   **Note 1 (Ding)**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **buzzer**, Frequency to **800**, and Duty to **50**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.5** seconds.
        *   **Note 2 (Dong)**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **buzzer**, Frequency to **600**, and Duty to **50**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** seconds.
        *   **Silence**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Pin to **buzzer** and Duty to **0**.
5.  **Anti-Spam Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.05** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: The user taps the doorbell button.
2.  **Step 1**: The buzzer plays an 800Hz tone for half a second.
3.  **Step 2**: The buzzer immediately switches to 600Hz for one second.
4.  **End**: The buzzer falls silent and waits for the next press.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = PWM(Pin(15))

while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Ding (High Note)
        buzzer.freq(800)
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
        time.sleep(0.5)
        
        # Dong (Low Note)
        buzzer.freq(600)
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
        time.sleep(1.0)
        
        # Stop sound
        buzzer.duty_u16(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Overlapping Notes**: If you forget the `duty(0)` at the end, the second note ("Dong") will continue playing forever after the button is pressed once.
*   **Holding the Button**: If the user holds the button down, the sequence will loop repeatedly. You can fix this by adding a "wait until button = 0" block at the end of the sequence.
*   **Low Frequencies**: Setting a frequency lower than 50Hz might cause the buzzer to vibrate mechanically without producing a clear sound.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Triple Chime**: Add a third note (e.g., 400Hz) to create a "Ding-Dong-Ding" pattern.
*   **Visual Chime**: Flash an LED (GP14) in sync with the notes (e.g., Red for Ding, Blue for Dong).
*   **Volume Control**: Adjust the Duty Cycle (e.g., 5% instead of 50%) to make a "quiet mode" doorbell.

## Project 0065: Interactive Doorball

### 2. Learning Objective
Combine fixed and variable timing. The bell rings for a minimum of 2 seconds on a short tap but continues as long as the button is held.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Minimum Duration**: Ensuring a short input creates a substantial output.
*   **State Extension**: Using a while loop to extend an action based on continuous input.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Hybrid Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Passive Sound Output |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`controls_whileUntil`** (while)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **buzzer**: Pin object (simple ON/OFF) or PWM object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`** and **`buzzer`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`buzzer`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Check Trigger**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
4.  **Hybrid Alert Logic**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   **Phase 1 (Minimum Pulse)**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **buzzer** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
        *   **Phase 2 (Hold Extension)**: From **Smart IO**, drag **`controls_whileUntil`**. Use the gear icon to set it to **while**.
        *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
        *   **Action**: Wait **0.1** seconds (keeps the light/sound on while held).
        *   **Phase 3 (Silence)**: After the while loop, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **buzzer** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Polling Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.05** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Short Press**: You tap the button for 0.1s. The buzzer rings for 2s then stops.
2.  **Long Press**: You hold the button for 5s. The buzzer rings for 2s (Phase 1) + 3s (Phase 2) = 5s total.
3.  **End**: Releasing the button after Phase 1 terminates the sound.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Minimum run time
        buzzer.value(1)
        time.sleep(2)
        
        # Extended hold time
        while btn.value() == 1:
            time.sleep(0.1)
            
        # Turning off
        buzzer.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Stuck in Loop**: If your button is defective and stays "High" forever, the buzzer will never turn off. This is why a "Max Duration" check is often added in real products.
*   **Passive vs Active**: This project uses simple Digital Write (ON/OFF). If you use a passive buzzer, it will just click. Use an active buzzer or return to the `PWM` frequency blocks from Project 0064.
*   **Logic Order**: If the `while` loop is placed before the 2s wait, the short tap wouldn't trigger the minimum duration.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Emergency Brake**: Add a second button (GP11) that immediately stops the doorbell sequence regardless of how long the first button is held.
*   **Timeout Safety**: Add a variable to ensure the bell never rings for more than 10 seconds, even if the button is held.
*   **Fading Decay**: If using PWM, slowly decrease the frequency or duty cycle at the end of the press for a more natural sound.

## Project 0066: Smart Doorball Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Pattern recognition through counting. Create a doorbell that only "unlocks" (Green LED) if the button is pressed exactly 3 times quickly.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Variable Incrementing**: Counting events.
*   **Timed Reset**: Clearing a counter if an action isn't completed within a window.
*   **Conditional Access**: Comparing a count to a target value.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Green LED (Lock)**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Pattern Input (Pull-Down) |
| **Link LED** | GP14 | Success Indicator |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Audio Feedback |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`change [variable] by [value]`** (math_change)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **lock**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **knocks**: Number variable used to count button presses.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`lock`**, and **`knocks`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`lock`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`knocks`** to **0**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Detect Press**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
4.  **Count & Wait**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`change [knocks] by 1`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.3** seconds (Debounce and wait for next knock).
5.  **Pattern Verification**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag another **`controls_if`** block below the first one.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`knocks`** equals **3**.
    *   **Action (Access Granted)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **lock** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **lock** and State to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Variables**, set **`knocks`** back to **0**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: User knocks three times quickly.
2.  **Counting**: Each knock increments the `knocks` variable.
3.  **Logic**: Once the count hits 3, the "secret" condition is met.
4.  **Output**: The green LED turns on for 2 seconds to simulate a door unlocking.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
lock = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
knocks = 0

while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        knocks += 1
        time.sleep(0.3) # Wait for next knock
        
    if knocks == 3:
        # Secret code success
        lock.value(1)
        time.sleep(2)
        lock.value(0)
        knocks = 0 # Reset for next time
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Accidental Counts**: Without the 0.3s wait, a single long press might be counted as 10 knocks.
*   **No Time Out**: In this simple version, if you knock twice now and once tomorrow, the door will open. Real systems reset the counter if no knock is heard for 1 second.
*   **Counting Past Target**: If you knock 4 times, the code (as written above) would open the door on the 3rd knock and ignoring the 4th might cause weirdness next time.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Timeout Reset**: Add logic so that if the button isn't pressed for 2 seconds, the `knocks` variable resets to 0 automatically.
*   **Wrong Code Penalty**: If the count goes over 3, flash a Red LED to indicate a failed attempt.
*   **Rhythmic Knock**: Use the time between knocks (using `ticks_ms`) to require a "Shave and a Haircut" rhythm.

---*   **led** (Pin)
*   **buzzer** (PWM)
*   **count** (Number)
*   **last_time** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP10** (Input/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Output) -> `led`.
    *   From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (PWM) -> `buzzer`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Initialize Variables**:
    *   From **Variables**, set `count` to 0.
    *   From **Variables**, set `last_time` to 0.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Register Press**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `btn` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        *   From **Variables**, change `count` by 1.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_freq_duty`. Set `buzzer` to **1000** Hz, **50** %.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_duty`. Set `buzzer` to **0** %.
        *   From **Variables**, set `last_time` to **Smart IO** `ticks_ms`.
        *   From **Loops**, `while` `btn` is HIGH:
            *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s** (Wait for release).
5.  **Check Success**:
    *   From **Logic**, drag `controls_if`. Condition: `count` >= 3.
    *   **Then**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set `led` to **HIGH** (Unlock).
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_freq_duty`. Set `buzzer` to **2000** Hz, **50** %.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_duty`. Set `buzzer` to **0** %.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set `led` to **LOW**.
        *   From **Variables**, set `count` to 0.
    *   **Timeout Check**:
        *   From **Logic**, drag `controls_if`. Condition: (`ticks_ms` - `last_time`) > 1000.
        *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `count` to 0 (Reset if too slow).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Locked.
2.  **Attempt 1**: Tap. (Count=1). Beep.
3.  **Attempt 2**: Tap. (Count=2). Beep.
4.  **Attempt 3**: Tap. (Count=3).
5.  **Success**: Access Granted (Green Light). count resets.
6.  **Fail**: If you wait > 1s between taps, count resets to 0.

### 10. Generated Code
``python
import machine
import time

btn = machine.Pin(10, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.OUT)
buzzer = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))
buzzer.freq(1000)

count = 0
last_time = 0

while True:
    if btn.value():
        count += 1
        # Beep
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768); time.sleep(0.1); buzzer.duty_u16(0)
        last_time = time.ticks_ms()
        # Debounce
        while btn.value(): time.sleep(0.05)
        
    if count >= 3:
        # Unlock
        led.value(1)
        buzzer.duty_u16(32768); time.sleep(0.5); buzzer.duty_u16(0) # Success tone
        time.sleep(2)
        led.value(0)
        count = 0
        
    # Timeout
    if time.ticks_diff(time.ticks_ms(), last_time) > 1000 and count > 0:
        count = 0 
        print("Timeout reset")
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
``

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Debounce is Critical**: Without `while btn.value()`, one human press looks like 50 machine presses, unlocking instantly.
*   **Smart IO**: 1 second is tight. Maybe allow 2 seconds.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Pattern**: Short-Short-Long (SOS) knock.
*   **Penalty**: Red Light / Alarm if wrong code entered.
    *   From **Logic**, drag `controls_if`. Condition: (`pressCount` > 0) AND (`time.ticks_ms()` - `lastPressTime` > 2000).
    *   Then:
        *   From **Variables**, set `pressCount` to 0 (Reset).
        *   From **Smart IO**, **Pulse** GP15 twice (Error beep).

## Project 0067: Doorball Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
State monitoring with temporal logic. Use a Reed switch to detect an open door and trigger an alert if it remains open past a 10-second grace period.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Reed Switch (Magnetic)**: Using magnetism to detect physical proximity (open/closed states).
*   **Time-Delayed Triggers**: Delaying an action until a specific condition has persisted for X seconds.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Reed Switch + Magnet**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Reed Switch** | GP10 | Magnetic Sensor (Pull-Up) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Alarm Indicator |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] > [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`get current time (ms)`** (ticks_ms)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`true`** / **`false`** (boolean)

### 7. Variables
*   **reed**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_UP)`.
*   **buzzer**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **open_time**: Number variable recording the exact millisecond the door opened.
*   **is_open**: Boolean variable tracking whether the door is currently open.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`reed`**, **`buzzer`**, **`open_time`**, and **`is_open`**.
    *   Set **`reed`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_UP)`**.
    *   Set **`buzzer`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`is_open`** to **`false`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Reed State Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** (with **else**).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **reed** equals **1** (Magnet Gone = Door Open).
    *   **Action (If - Door Open)**:
        *   Drag another **`controls_if`**. Check if **`is_open`** is **false**.
        *   **Inside**: Set **`open_time`** to **`get current time (ms)`**. Set **`is_open`** to **`true`**.
        *   Drag a third **`controls_if`**. Check if (**`get current time (ms)`** - **`open_time`**) > **10000**.
        *   **Inside**: Set **buzzer** to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - Door Closed)**:
        *   From **Variables**, set **`is_open`** to **`false`**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set **buzzer** to **LOW (0)**.
4.  **Scan Rate**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Closed**: Magnet is near. Reed = 0. Alarm = Off.
2.  **Opening**: Magnet leaves. Reed = 1. The Pico records the `open_time`.
3.  **Ticking**: If the door stays open for 10,001ms, the alarm triggers.
4.  **Closing**: Closing the door at 9 seconds prevents the alarm. Closing it at 11 seconds stops the alarm.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
# Pull-Up means it reads 0 when closed (magnet present) and 1 when open
reed = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_UP)
buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
is_open = False
open_time = 0

while True:
    if reed.value() == 1:
        if not is_open:
            open_time = time.ticks_ms()
            is_open = True
        
        # Check if 10 seconds (10000ms) have passed
        if time.ticks_diff(time.ticks_ms(), open_time) > 10000:
            buzzer.value(1) # ALARM
    else:
        is_open = False # Key reset
        buzzer.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Pull-Up Logic**: If you use `Pin.IN` without `PULL_UP`, the wire will act as an antenna and trigger the alarm randomly.
*   **Timer Conflict**: If you don't use the `is_open` boolean to lock the `open_time`, the timer will keep resetting every 0.1s, and the alarm will never go off!
*   **Millisecond Math**: Remember that `10000` is 10 seconds. Using `10` would trigger the alarm in 0.01 seconds.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Multiple Doors**: Add a second Reed switch on GP11. Trigger the alarm if *either* door stays open for too long.
*   **Beep Frequency**: Make the buzzer beep slowly starting at 5 seconds, increasing in speed until the 10-second full alarm.
*   **Log Violations**: Use `pico_log` to count how many "Door Propped" events occur in a day.

## Project 0068: The Doorball Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Randomization and logic branches. Use the `random` module to create a doorbell that gives a "Treat" (nice tone) or a "Trick" (scary tone) at random.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Pseudo-Random Number Generation**: Generating values that are statistically random using algorithms.
*   **Outcome Selection**: Using a variable to decide which block of code to execute.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Random Trigger (Pull-Down) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Passive Sound Output |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`controls_if`** (if / else)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`[X] = [Y]`** (logic_compare)
*   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`random integer from 1 to 100`** (math_random_int)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`** (pulse Pin ...)
*   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`** (wait)
*   From **Variables**, drag **`set [variable] to`** (variable assignment)
*   From **Text**, drag **`""`** (text string for direct Pin definition)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **buzzer**: PWM object initialized as `PWM(Pin(15))`.
*   **outcome**: Number variable used to store the random result (1 or 2).

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`buzzer`**, and **`outcome`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`buzzer`** to **`PWM(Pin(15))`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Detect Press**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
4.  **Roll the Dice**:
    *   Inside the **If** block:
        *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [outcome] to`**.
        *   From **Logic & Math**, drag **`random integer from 1 to 2`** (math_random_int).
5.  **Calculate Result**:
        *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** (with **else**).
        *   **Condition**: Check if **`outcome`** equals **1**.
        *   **Action (If - Treat)**:
            *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Frequency to **1000** and Duty to **50**.
        *   **Action (Else - Trick)**:
            *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Frequency to **100** and Duty to **50**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **0.5** seconds.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_pwm`**. Set Duty to **0** (Silence).
6.  **Debounce Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.5** seconds (to avoid multiple triggers from one press).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Interact**: A visitor presses the button.
2.  **Randomize**: The Pico generates a number.
3.  **Evaluate**:
    *   If 1: A pleasant 1000Hz chime plays.
    *   If 2: A spooky 100Hz buzz plays.
4.  **Reset**: The buzzer stops, and the system waits 0.5s before allowing the next "roll".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time
import random

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = PWM(Pin(15))

while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Generate 1 or 2
        outcome = random.randint(1, 2)
        
        if outcome == 1:
            # Treat: High ascending
            buzzer.freq(1000)
            buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
        else:
            # Trick: Low buzz
            buzzer.freq(100)
            buzzer.duty_u16(32768)
            
        time.sleep(0.5)
        buzzer.duty_u16(0) # Silence
        
        # Long debounce for game feel
        time.sleep(0.5)
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Math Range**: If you set the random block from 1 to 100 but only check for `outcome == 1`, you will get the "Trick" 99% of the time. Ensure the range matches your outcomes.
*   **Duty Cycle Reset**: If the buzzer is stuck on the "Trick" or "Treat" tone, you probably forgot the `duty(0)` block at the end of the sequence.
*   **Predictable Start**: Without seeding (Project 0071), the "random" sequence is the same every time the Pico restarts.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Mega Odds**: Set random from 1 to 10. Pay a "Jackpot" song (multiple notes) only if the outcome is 10.
*   **Visual Feedback**: Link a Green LED to the Treat and a Red LED to the Trick.
*   **Three Strikes**: Use a counter (Project 0066) to give two treats then a guaranteed trick on the third press.

## Project 0069: Automated Doorball

### 2. Learning Objective
Touchless interaction with verification. Use an ultrasonic sensor to detect a visitor and require them to stay within range for 2 seconds to prevent false triggers (Dwell Logic).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Proximity Detection**: Triggering an event based on spatial distance.
*   **Dwell Verification**: Confirming an input is intentional by requiring it to persist over time.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Ultrasonic Sensor (HC-SR04)**
*   **1x Passive Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Trig** | GP16 | Distance Trigger Pulse |
| **Echo** | GP17 | Distance Measurement Echo |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Passive Sound Output |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `controls_if`** (if)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `logic_compare`** (compare)
*   **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_distance`** (get distance cm)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)
*   **from Variables, drag `set variable to`** (variable assignment)
*   **from Text, drag `text`** (text string)

### 7. Variables
*   **dist**: Number variable representing the measured distance in cm.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variable **`dist`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Initial Detection**:
    *   From **Variables**, drag **`set [dist] to`**. From **Smart Sensors**, drag **`get distance cm`**. Set Trig to **16** and Echo to **17**.
4.  **Verification Logic**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** block.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`dist`** < **50**.
    *   **Action (Verify Dwell)**:
        *   Inside the **If** block:
            *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **2.0** seconds.
            *   From **Variables**, set **`dist`** to **`get distance cm`** (Check again).
            *   Drag another **`controls_if`**. Condition: Check if **`dist`** < **50**.
            *   **Action (Confirmed Visit)**:
                *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
                *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.5** seconds.
                *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **15** and State to **LOW (0)**.
                *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **5.0** seconds (Anti-spam cooldown).
5.  **Scan Frequency**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.1** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Scan**: The Pico measures distance 10 times per second.
2.  **Alert**: If someone stands closer than 50cm, the "Dwell" timer starts.
3.  **Confirm**: If they are still there 2 seconds later, the bell rings.
4.  **Reset**: A 5-second cooldown ensures the bell doesn't keep ringing if they never leave.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
from pico_sensor import Ultrasonic
import time

# Initialization
sensor = Ultrasonic(trig_pin=16, echo_pin=17)
buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Initial distance check
    if sensor.distance_cm() < 50:
        # Wait 2 seconds to see if they stick around
        time.sleep(2)
        
        # Verify they are still there
        if sensor.distance_cm() < 50:
            print("Verified Visitor!")
            buzzer.value(1)
            time.sleep(0.5)
            buzzer.value(0)
            
            # Prevent rapid re-triggering
            time.sleep(5)
            
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **False Triggers**: Pets or shifting shadows might trigger the 2s wait. The verification step (`if get_dist() < 50` again) is essential to filter these out.
*   **Sensor Glitch**: If the sensor reads "0" constantly, ensure it has a stable 3.3V power supply (some cheap HC-SR04s require 5V to work reliably).
*   **Cooldown Bypass**: If you remove the 5s cooldown, the doorbell will sound like a siren if the visitor stands at the door for a long chat.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Distance Tones**: Change the pitch of the buzzer based on how close the visitor is standing.
*   **Away Message**: If the visitor leaves *during* the 2-second wait, play a short "Goodbye" beep.
*   **Day/Night Mode**: Only allow touchless ringing during daylight hours (requires LDR from Project 0056).

## Project 0070: Mastering Doorball

### 2. Learning Objective
System decoupling. Simulate a wireless transmitter and receiver by separating the input logic (transmitter) and the output logic (receiver) using a shared boolean variable.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Decoupling**: Organizing code so that independent systems communicate through shared data (variables) rather than direct control.
*   **Signaling**: Using a boolean variable as a virtual "broadcast" from one part of the code to another.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Push Button**
*   **1x White LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Wireless Transmitter (Pull-Down) |
| **LED** | GP15 | Wireless Receiver |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `controls_if`** (if / else)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `logic_boolean`** (true/false)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)
*   **from Variables, drag `set variable to`** (variable assignment)
*   **from Text, drag `text`** (text string)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **signal**: Boolean variable representing the "wireless" transmission status.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`led`**, and **`signal`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`signal`** to **`false`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Transmitter Logic (Input Processor)**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** (with **else**).
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
    *   **Action (If - Button Pressed)**:
        *   From **Variables**, set **`signal`** to **`true`**.
    *   **Action (Else - Idle)**:
        *   From **Variables**, set **`signal`** to **`false`**.
4.  **Receiver Logic (Output Processor)**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag another **`controls_if`** (with **else**) below the first one.
    *   **Condition**: Check if **`signal`** is **`true`**.
    *   **Action (If - Signal Received)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   **Action (Else - No Signal)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.01** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Isolation**: The "Transmitter" section only cares about the button and setting the `signal` variable.
2.  **Simulation**: The `signal` variable acts as a radio bridge.
3.  **Observation**: The "Receiver" section only watches the `signal` variable and reacts by turning the LED on or off.
4.  **Benefits**: This makes it easy to add more receivers (multiple LEDs watching the same variable) or more transmitters (buttons) later.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
signal = False

while True:
    # Part 1: TRANSMITTER logic
    if btn.value() == 1:
        signal = True
    else:
        signal = False
        
    # Part 2: RECEIVER logic
    if signal == True:
        led.value(1)
    else:
        led.value(0)
        
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Variable Naming**: If you misspell the `signal` variable in one section, the "bridge" is broken and the LED will never light up.
*   **Over-polling**: 0.01s is fast. If your code is complex, this might cause lag. 0.05s is usually enough for human-reaction projects.
*   **Logical Loop**: If you accidentally try to read the LED state to set the signal, you create a feedback loop that might behave erratically.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Receiver**: Add a second LED on GP14. Make it also check the `signal` variable. Now one button controls two "wireless" units.
*   **Delayed Relay**: Make the Receiver wait 1 second *after* the signal becomes True before turning on the LED.
*   **Inverted Receiver**: Make the LED turn ON when the signal is False and OFF when the signal is True.

# Batch 8: Reaction Game 1

## Project 0071: Introduction to Reaction Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Sequential states and input gating. Create a racing start system where a button press is only acknowledged after a 3-second "Wait" period signaled by a Red LED.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Gating**: Ignoring inputs until a specific condition or time has passed.
*   **Status Indicators**: Using distinct LEDs to communicate the current "state" to the user.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x Red LED**
*   **1x Green LED**
*   **1x Push Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Player Input (Pull-Down) |
| **Red LED** | GP14 | "Wait" Signal |
| **Green LED** | GP15 | "GO!" Signal |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `controls_if`** (if)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `logic_negate`** (not)
*   **from Loops, drag `controls_whileUntil`** (while)
*   **from Loops, drag `controls_repeat_ext`** (repeat times)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_toggle`** (toggle Pin ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)
*   **from Variables, drag `set variable to`** (variable assignment)
*   **from Text, drag `text`** (text string)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **red**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **grn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`red`**, and **`grn`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`red`** to **`Pin(14, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   Set **`grn`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **The Wait State**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **red** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **grn** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **3.0** seconds.
4.  **The GO State**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **red** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **grn** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
5.  **Input Monitoring**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`controls_whileUntil`**. Use the gear icon to set it to **while**.
    *   **Condition**: Drag a **`logic_negate`** (not) block. Inside, check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
    *   **Action**: Wait **0.01** seconds (Small loop to block progress until pressed).
6.  **Celebrate Victory**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`controls_repeat_ext`**. Set to **5** times.
        *   Inside: From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_toggle`** for Pin **grn**. Wait **0.1** seconds.
7.  **Reset Delay**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, wait **1.0** second before starting the next "Wait" phase.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Ready**: Red light turns on. The Pico ignores everything for 3 seconds.
2.  **Go**: Green light turns on. The code enters the second `while` loop.
3.  **Listen**: The code stays in the loop until the button is pressed.
4.  **Finish**: Once pressed, the green light flashes, and the cycle repeats.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)
grn = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Phase 1: Wait
    red.value(1)
    grn.value(0)
    time.sleep(3)
    
    # Phase 2: GO!
    red.value(0)
    grn.value(1)
    
    # Phase 3: Loop until pressed
    while btn.value() == 0:
        time.sleep(0.01)
        
    # Phase 4: Win Animation
    for _ in range(10): # 5 cycles of Toggle
        grn.toggle()
        time.sleep(0.1)
        
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Button Spamming**: If you press the button during the 3-second Red light, nothing happens. This is intentional but might confuse players expecting immediate feedback.
*   **Toggle Count**: Toggling 5 times results in only 2.5 full blinks. Increase to 10 for 5 full On/Off cycles.
*   **Logic Window**: Using a `while btn == 0` loop blocks the entire program. This is fine for this game, but in more complex apps, it stops other sensors from working.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Random Wait**: Instead of exactly 3 seconds, use the `random integer` block to wait between 2 and 5 seconds to make it harder.
*   **False Start Penalty**: If the button is pressed *while* Red is On, turn on a Buzzer (GP15) and restart the loop early.
*   **Reaction Timer**: Use `ticks_ms` to calculate and print how many milliseconds it took you to press the button after the green light appeared.

## Project 0072: Blinking Reaction Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Precision timing in high-speed loops. Program an LED to blink extremely fast (0.1s window) and trigger a "Win" animation only if the button is pressed exactly during the On-phase.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Temporal Precision**: Mastering intervals below 1 second.
*   **Input Polling (Windowed)**: Checking for user interaction within a specific, short-lived time frame.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1x LED**
*   **1x Push Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP10 | Reaction Input (Pull-Down) |
| **LED** | GP15 | Reaction Target |
| **Ground** | GND | Common Ground reference |

### 6. Blocks Used
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_forever`** (forever do)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `controls_if`** (if / else)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `logic_compare`** (compare)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_ticks_ms`** (get current time)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`** (read Pin)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`** (set Pin to ...)
*   **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`** (wait)
*   **from Variables, drag `set variable to`** (variable assignment)
*   **from Text, drag `text`** (text string)
*   **from Logic & Math, drag `logic_boolean`** (true/false)

### 7. Variables
*   **btn**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`.
*   **led**: Pin object initialized as `Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`.
*   **caught**: Boolean variable used to track if the "catch" was successful.
*   **window_start**: Number variable recording the start of the current blink.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware Variables**:
    *   Create variables **`btn`**, **`led`**, **`caught`**, and **`window_start`**.
    *   Set **`btn`** to **`Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)`**.
    *   Set **`led`** to **`Pin(15, Pin.OUT)`**.
    *   **Snap** into the **`start`** block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag the **`pico_forever`** block.
3.  **Trigger Blink**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Variables**, set **`caught`** to **false**.
    *   From **Variables**, set **`window_start`** to **`get current time (ms)`**.
4.  **Precision Check (0.1s Window)**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag a **`controls_whileUntil`** (while).
    *   **Condition**: Check if (**`get current time (ms)`** - **`window_start`**) < **100**.
    *   **Action**:
        *   Inside, drag a **`controls_if`**. Check if **`pico_gpio_read`** for **btn** equals **1**.
        *   **If True**: Set **`caught`** to **true**. Use the **`break`** block (from Loops) to exit the while loop instantly.
5.  **Evaluate Interaction**:
    *   From **Logic & Math**, drag a **`controls_if`** (with **else**).
    *   **Action (If - Success)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **2.0** seconds (Light stays On to congratulate you).
    *   **Action (Else - Fail)**:
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **led** and State to **LOW (0)**.
        *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set to **0.5** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Cycle**: Every 0.6 seconds (0.1 window + 0.5 rest), the light flashes.
2.  **Detect**: The Pico polls the button thousands of times during that 100ms window.
3.  **Score**: If a press is registered exactly while the LED is On, the boolean flips to True.
4.  **Feedback**: A successful catch freezes the light for 2 seconds.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Initialization
btn = Pin(10, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # 1. Turn LED on and start timer
    led.value(1)
    caught = False
    window_start = time.ticks_ms()
    
    # 2. Check for button press for 100ms
    while time.ticks_diff(time.ticks_ms(), window_start) < 100:
        if btn.value() == 1:
            caught = True
            break
            
    # 3. Handle Result
    if caught:
        # Success! Wait 2 seconds
        time.sleep(2)
    else:
        # Miss! Turn off and rest 0.5s
        led.value(0)
        time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **The Sleep Mistake**: If you use `time.sleep(0.1)` between turning the LED on and checking the button, you will miss the press. You must use a `while` loop with a timer check to poll the button *during* the blink.
*   **Button Release**: If you hold the button down, you will win every time. A "Fair Play" addition would be forcing the user to release the button *before* the next blink starts.
*   **Debounce jitter**: Tapping too hard can cause "bounce". The `break` block helps, but the 2s wait after a win is the best debounce.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Level Up**: Decrease the 100ms window by 10ms every time the player wins.
*   **Buzz on Miss**: Add a buzzer (GP15) that sounds a low "error" tone when the player misses the light.
*   **Multi-LED Roulette**: Have 3 LEDs (GP13, 14, 15) and try to catch a specific one as they cycle.
